One line

What is being decided

Moved out of OI-104 (WI-788's checkpoint, its question Q-4) at the owner's direction, 2026-10-04: "You can do whatever is desired to build up but the router isn't active yet so it will still be an open item to followup on when I have it".

WHAT IS HELD. WI-788's design note (chapter 2 section 5) ships a FreeLLMAPI route row only for a pinned model id whose chain, declared in the account home's FreeLLMAPI configuration, holds that one model, so the row's family is knowable; a router alias such as auto is never a row. The one live call the owner authorized (OI-104 Q-3) checks that the served model equals the pinned one. Neither can happen without a running endpoint: the 2026-10-04 probes verified the per-route OpenCode config (opencode models freellmapi lists freellmapi/auto), and a run fails at the connection to 127.0.0.1:3001.

WHAT IS NOT HELD. S788-routes builds everything that needs no endpoint: the FreeLLMAPI account template and OpenCode config, the Grok row, and the refusal of a FreeLLMAPI row with no declared one-model chain.

WHAT TO RULE, once the router runs: the endpoint (a local install at :3001, or hosted), the model ids to pin, and whether FreeLLMAPI can declare a one-model chain per pinned id.

Blast radius

Only the FreeLLMAPI route row and its live call (part of S788-routes' acceptance) wait on this; the rest of S788-routes and every other S788-* row proceed. Its queued placeholder WI-795 reads blocked.

  • (b) PROVISION WITHOUT ONE-MODEL CHAINS: no FreeLLMAPI row ships (the note's rule), and the template stays for a later router.
  • (c) DROP FREELLMAPI: the template and config are retired and no row is planned.

SR-006Gate/tier harness enforces required stepsre-accept owed

Requirement

The delivered harness shall run the required steps of the gate that must next be passed (the strictness selector cached in docs/stage) and fail that gate when a required tool is missing, reporting SKIP(missing) rather than silently passing — and, on a claimed work branch (one whose history holds a docs/work/active/<branch>/ claim), SKIP its generated-artifact freshness steps with a stated trunk-lane reason, generated artifacts being trunk-only, while every non-freshness step still runs.

Rationale

Realizes SN-004 (gates enforce their stage's checks), SN-008 (no false pass) and the dissolved edge expectation that a missing tool fails, never skips. A sanctioned SKIP class of this harness's own step plan is not a second crossing observable, it is the same one qualified. The skip is not a weakening: a work branch cannot commit generated artifacts at all (concurrency-restructure §5.2), so demanding their freshness there would force the very conflict the rule deletes; the skip is reported, never silent, and any signal ambiguity resolves toward the full trunk checks.

SR-028Coordinator typed outcomesre-accept owed

Requirement

The delivered coordinator shall end each session in a typed outcome code, guard a zero-commit HEAD, and report an all-ERROR stall as an unavailable agent rather than a work stall.

Rationale

Realizes SN-006 and the dissolved edge expectations that a repository with no commits yet (HEAD absent) does not crash the loop and that an all-ERROR run is an unavailable agent rather than a work stall — a coordinator ending in an untyped code forces its caller to parse prose; a session that produced no commit still has a HEAD to report, so without a guard an empty run is indistinguishable from a productive one in the record it leaves; and an all-ERROR run reported as a work stall sends an operator looking for a blocked work item that does not exist. Treating an agent error as a stall was rejected for that reason: an unreachable agent and a blocked item need different responses, so they need different outcomes.

SR-031Declared-policy readers agreere-accept owed

Requirement

Every delivered enforcer of a declared policy dial shall read the same value for that dial as every other enforcer — one parse, one answer, across the hooks, the loop and the checkers alike.

Rationale

Realizes SN-028's one readable policy home. A policy answer calculated more than once can diverge across enforcers, leaving the operator unable to know which value governs; one parse gives every enforcer the same answer. SR-137 owns whether a declaration is ambiguous or invalid, while this row owns agreement among the enforcers that consume a valid declaration.

A privacy gate declaration outside the policy home's policy table reads gate on for every enforcer; a privacy reviewer declaration outside that table reads reviewer required for every enforcer, and an unreadable declaration refuses at the hooks rather than reading a default.

SR-RefsSR-031
TitleShared declared-policy parse
DetailAll policy enforcers, including the agent layer, obtain a declared policy answer from one parsed representation. A privacy gate declaration the parse holds outside the policies table reads as gate on, whatever its value or any direct or legacy gate declaration; a privacy reviewer declaration it holds there reads as reviewer required, whatever its value or any direct or legacy reviewer declaration. A name inside a string declares nothing. An unreadable or unparseable declaration is a distinct outcome, never a default. Only the exact opt-down value directly under the policies table opts the unwired reviewer down; a copy elsewhere or an unreadable declaration requires the reviewer, and the legacy record answers only when that key is absent. The hook answer is one of: the gate off; on with the unwired reviewer required; on with it warned; a mixed declaration; or an unreadable declaration, as IF-298 carries. The hooks no longer read a declared line themselves and instead reach it through that answer.

SR-043Subagent spawn gatere-accept owed

Requirement

While an unattended run is in progress, the delivered subagent-spawn gate shall refuse or defer subagent spawns per the declared subagent-gate dial in the single policy declaration home (off|ask|deny; absent=off; the legacy one-word file is still read through the migration window), honor a launcher-set SUBAGENT_GATE=allow override the model cannot set, and fail open on any error so a broken gate never wedges the tools.

Rationale

Realizes SN-006 (a walk-away run stays safe — bounded, supervised fan-out with the override held by the operator, not the model) and SN-012 (opt-in: absent/off is a vacuous allow, so a non-adopter pays nothing). Supervision, not security — a model that can edit files can remove the hook (adapted from stop-subagent-fanout, MIT; spec docs/archive/INTEGRATION_PLAN.md Phase 4). C-SEC-2 asks that the irreversible actions an unattended run can take be enumerated, each naming the dial or operator authority that permits it — which is this row exactly, for the one action that starts further actors. A direct hit, and the reason this row is not accretion: its parentage was real and lived only in SN-006's `why` cell until the safety half moved into that need's normative text at this same pass. WHY THE FAIL-OPEN ARM IS DELIBERATE: SN-006 asks that the unattended layer keep moving and surface to the operator only where it cannot proceed, so a gate that wedges every tool on its own bug is the failure this clause was written against; what an error never relaxes is the operator-held override itself, which the model cannot set.

A pure decision maps a tool call's name, the declared gate policy and an allow override to allow, ask, deny or defer, deferring every call that names neither of the agent tool's two subagent-spawn tools. Run as the agent tool's pre-tool-use hook, the gate reads the call's payload from standard input, reads the gate dial from the process configuration's checks table (falling back to the legacy one-word declaration through the migration window) and applies the allow override set in its environment. SR-046Declared runnable capabilities reachable from the repository rootre-accept owed

Requirement

The launchers at the repository root shall expose every declared runnable capability consistently to an interactive operator, to a direct caller naming one capability, and to automated discovery - from a single declaration, on Windows and POSIX alike.

Rationale

Realizes SN-001 - the evaluator's rungs make running the product never depend on recalling a command, and the capability set is declared once in docs/stack.ini rather than hard-wired and duplicated across the platform launchers. ONE OBLIGATION, not a menu specification: the row states the one capability-level decision - every declared capability is reachable, the same way, from one declaration, by three kinds of caller - because numbering, listing format, declaration grammar, fallback text and exit handling fail independently, which makes an enumeration of them a decomposition wearing a requirement's clothes. Those are LLR-047's and the acceptance's.

LLR-IDLLR-047
SR-RefsSR-046
TitleRun capability menu reader
DetailThe run menu reads the run section of the stack profile into a list of name, command and description in declaration order, with no interpolation, so a percent sign in a value is literal, and keeping each name's case; a name's description row attaches to its command, and a description row naming no command, or a command with an empty value, is dropped. Its list form prints one line per capability: the name, a tab and the description. A capability named on the command line is launched by exact name, an unknown name exiting 2, and the launch runs the declared line through the shell and passes the child's exit code straight through. The launch puts first on the search path a directory whose bare python resolves to the menu's own interpreter: on Windows that interpreter's own directory, and on POSIX a per-launch temporary directory of executable shims that run its exact path. On Windows the command processor searches the line's working directory before the search path, so a python executable there, at the repository root or in a directory the line enters, runs instead and is outside this guarantee; with no interpreter there, the prepended directory governs the search. On POSIX that directory also makes bare python3 exact, and a line meant to run on Windows declares python; the POSIX construction is a shim rather than a link so a virtual environment keeps its configuration. With no argument it prints the numbered menu, reads a pick from standard input, and returns without hanging w… [929 more chars — read the registry row]
RationaleThe command being run is the user's own declared line, so running it through the shell is the same trust boundary the declaration already establishes rather than a new one. Safety at end-of-file is load-bearing: a menu that blocks on a closed standard input turns an unattended invocation into a hang, which is the failure this launcher exists to avoid.

SR-139Acceptance as an ordinal over a derived spine stagere-accept owed

Requirement

The kit shall express operator acceptance authority as a cumulative level naming the highest spine tier an operator still accepts, compared against a separately derived SPINE STAGE via a declared, auditable rung ordering — every unreadable or out-of-range input resolving toward MORE operator involvement.

Rationale

The retired three-value enum could not express "TCs are operator-held but LLRs are not", and four tables each re-interpreted the same word with its own fail-safe direction. The failure that matters is a machine accepting something an operator meant to hold, so every degrade has to point the same way — including the ones that look like kindness: clamping an out-of-range level toward 0 reads as "nothing is operator-held" and disarms every hold in the repo. The crossing attribution: B-02 is unarguable - the acceptance level IS the authority input, and every fail-safe clause here describes how that input is read when it is broken. B-05 earns its place on a test, not on the fact that everything here is delivered content: the acceptance clause "Level N holds exactly the tiers the SHIPPED TEMPLATE documents" is a claim about the package's own contents agreeing with its behaviour, which is falsifiable against the package alone with no authority act performed - the same package-wide property class SR-031 occupies. The objection this had to survive is the measured one: B-05 already carries about four fifths of the layer's references, so adding a row to it needs an observable that is genuinely AT the package, not a default. This row has one. That the imbalance itself may be wrong is a separate question about the frame rather than about this row, and it is recorded where such questions belong.

SR-146Prompts are reviewable files with a per-session audit trailre-accept owed

Requirement

Every prompt the delivered loop launches or an attended launcher renders as a review or critique brief shall be a shipped, reviewable file with strictly filled slots — listed by digest in a freshness-gated generated catalogue, each loop session recording which template it used and the fingerprint of what it rendered to.

Rationale

Prose steers the sessions this loop launches and had been reviewable only by reading Python source, which makes the process trusted rather than inspectable. The audit trail is the part that makes the move useful rather than cosmetic: without a per-session digest, "which instruction did this session see" stays an inference. (The argv-vs-shell-string transport decision is design, and lives one tier down in LLR-163.) C-SEC-5 requires that content composed for dispatch to an external model runner carry a DECLARED inclusion rule rather than an implicit one — a prompt assembled inside source is an unreviewed egress path no write-side gate covers, and shipping it as a reviewable file with a digest is what makes that rule readable.

Phase6

SR-148Autonomous loop work selection: what it derives from, and in what orderre-accept owed

Requirement

The delivered loop content shall select exactly one work item for each assignment an unattended run admits from the repository's tracked registries and git history alone, in this order: ready adjudication rows first, as a stable partition applied at admission rather than by renumbering the ruled rank table; then unresolved handback records; then the earliest incomplete spine tier in SN-to-SR-to-LLR-to-TC order; then implementation work after test-case layout is complete — with the eligible set and its order deterministic, an item whose declared safety, policy or plan-mode inputs are missing, undeclared or contradictory failing closed for that item alone, operator holds applied only from the declared acceptance level, nothing admitted past an operator-held stop, no prose surface and no predefined track in the derivation, no hand-curated next-work or run-phase pointer surface shipped for any live instruction or executable surface to read, write, validate, generate or link, and the status surface a session reads generated and freshness-gated rather than hand-copied.

Rationale

Without one precedence rule, the same repository can resume into implementation while returned obligations or prerequisite requirement tiers remain unresolved. That produces work against stale intent and makes repeated resumes select different next actions. A recorded selection class, source record and hold decision provide replayable evidence of the choice. ONE SR PER (need, property): the loop's work-selection invariant — what the next work derives from, in what order, and that no hand-curated pointer surface participates in it — is stated here and nowhere else, so no second row can drift from it. An explicit three-way partition was rejected: the partition already existed textually and still produced duplicated acceptance. The no-pointer obligation is stated for a migrated repository as well as a fresh scaffold, and the migrated half is the one that matters most: a fresh scaffold never had the files to keep, while an upgraded repository can retain the retired authority files and still pass. Fan-out re-stamp: this row states one decision — what an unattended run's next work derives from and in what order — and its children are the successive stages of that one selection, not separable contracts; the width is the deliberate consolidation of three rows that stated the same invariant into one, so re-splitting to satisfy the bound would restore the duplication the consolidation removed.

SR-RefsSR-148
TitleDisposition adoption is validated once and recorded at close
DetailA disposition adoption is a fenced TOML block containing exactly one distinct, well-formed successor id. The disposed row is resolved from the title's one event token. For each adoption, validation accepts exactly one strict-UTF-8, parseable specification that is open in draft, queued or active state and whose supersedes value names the disposed row; it refuses every other state, unreadable or unparseable specification, missing title token, malformed fence or request, or unavailable listing. The close validates in the tree that becomes its commit parent and writes the validated ids, in request order, as the closed specification's record in the same rewrite as the close. A replay trusts that record without re-validating successor state or reading history; it joins recorded and drafted successors in one lineage operation, and refuses a request without a record, a record without a request, unequal request and record, a malformed record, or an unreadable merged specification. Every line whose text begins with a fence marker is a fence line; a disposition fence stands at column zero, opens only as TOML and closes only as a bare paired fence.
RationaleValidating at the close fixes the successor relation at the event that records it, while a durable record lets later replay preserve that relation without treating later successor state or unavailable history as authority.

SR-154Independent review routed across familiesre-accept owed

Requirement

The delivered loop content shall obtain, when unattended work reaches integration, each review or critique verdict the declared policy requires from a session that did not author the work — resolved per in-process phase and tier from the delivered agent registry's declared (family x model x tier) rows and only while the declared consent surface that turns managed selection on is present, drawn from a different model family wherever one is configured, degrading only to the documented same-family mode — with every selection logged before launch and a non-converging rework loop escalated through the declared acceptance level, and, where a claimed work item is marked for an in-lane single plan, refuse build output at its making or landing until a pass record binds its plan and Done-when.

Rationale

Realizes SN-026 (cross-family second opinions — every prior carrier of this contract demoted as routing machinery, so the sn_refs join forced the mint; SR-155 cites SN-026 too, for the planner pair, but only this row states the second opinion itself), SN-024 (an author cannot judge its own output) and SN-006. One row because routing, scheduling, scoring and escalation are one delivered contract — that the second opinion happens, independently, or its absence is documented — whose mechanics are the component details below it. The row also carries SN-026's DECLARATION and CONSENT half — `docs/agents.toml`'s pair-rows and the `docs/agents-enabled` surface whose presence turns managed selection on. That half had ZERO textual occurrence anywhere in the SR layer while `orphans=0` still held, because an orphan count proves a need has a citing row and never that the row carries the whole need. Managed selection is opt-in by consent rather than by configuration alone: a repository that declares families without dropping the surface has declared a capability, not turned one on, and the row now says so where a check can read it. fan-out re-stamp: the single plan's independent critique is one obligation carried by its briefs and filing and by its gate; the routing children remain separate.

SR-RefsSR-154;SR-156
TitleThe record a merge judges stays as its author committed it
RationaleEvery merge is judged from records - the verdict files, the Done-when checklist - and each of them could be changed by the lane being judged: a reviewer committing work beside its verdict, a later session rewriting an earlier round, a builder rewording its own Done-when. Nothing on a commit reliably names its role, but the coordinator's session log does, so the checks key on it and are re-derived at merge from what was committed rather than trusted from the loop. Reading a round as its session committed it removes the rewrite without adding a refusal. The Done-when claim rule warns rather than refuses because the intake mint still files its derived rows without a Done-when, and a refusal would park every one of them.

VerifiesSR-154;LLR-337;IF-295
LevelIntegration
MethodDrive a claimed single-plan lane through pre-commit, squash and landing checks before and after a pass record. Change the plan or Done-when, then tick a Done-when item; exercise absent, unreadable, malformed, other-row and other-digest records; alter a record's parent support; exercise every record home and a close; rebase and merge; vary the claimed planmode; and run trunk and a lane without a single-plan row. Exercise a non-string plan mark, a squashed tip no local branch contains, and a cited critique round past the cap. Run the shipped pre-commit step list on a lane with staged build output; refresh a partial closed lane by merging trunk with a compiled log and regenerated view, write a regenerated view outside a merge, and file a pass record whose critique reviewed another Done-when. Also run test_a_claimed_single_lane_with_the_trunk_undeclared_gets_one_hold_naming_it and test_a_branch_holding_no_claim_folder_is_judged_at_head_with_the_trunk_undeclared.
TierFull
ExpectedBuild output is held at each gate until a supported pass record binds it, and a changed plan or Done-when unbinds it while a tick does not. Unsupported records hold. Record homes and a valid close pass before a record. A rebase preserves a binding and a merge is judged only for its own changes. Only the lane's claimed copy controls the closed planmode set: absent, empty, none and dual do not hold; single and every other value do. Trunk and a lane without a single-plan row are not held. A non-string mark and an unattributed squash hold by name, and a critique round past the cap is no record. The shipped hook step list refuses unplanned build output by the gate's name. A log fragment, and a refresh merge's compiled log and regenerated views, pass before a record; a regenerated view written outside a merge is build output. A pass form or pass-record commit whose critique reviewed another Done-when digest is refused. A pass record renders and reads through one exact grammar; partial, extra-keyed or field-invalid records are malformed, and its delta is one typed line from none, within or beyond. A critique record outside its own plan home, or past the cap, is no record. With the trunk undeclared, a branch whose HEAD holds no claim folder is judged at HEAD; any other branch is refused naming the declaration.
AutomatedYes
StatusAccepted
Phase6

SR-156Bounded lanes narrowing to one gated landingre-accept owed

Requirement

The delivered loop content shall run exactly one work item per lane on bounded, mutually isolated lanes and narrow every lane back to the integration branch through one serial, fail-closed seam that runs the declared checks on the composed tree — parking a failing candidate and stopping the queue loudly, draining claiming to a clean merged stop under a declared pause, and reconstructing claim and queue state from version-control history alone at any crash boundary, never a half-integrated authoritative state.

Rationale

Realizes SN-027 WHOLE — ready work fans out across bounded parallel lanes and that fan-out narrows to one serialized gated seam before anything lands, in the need's own words — with SN-008 (the seam is fail-closed) and SN-025 (recovery from version-control history alone). ONE DECISION PER ROW: "serial integration is one delivered contract" is sound for the SEAM, but lane execution, claim authority and the trunk-side mint of work-item identity are not the seam, and a row stating the seam alone leaves SN-027's FIRST half — bounded lanes, isolated working trees, a worker ceiling — stated nowhere. This row therefore states the whole lane LIFECYCLE (claim, run isolated, close, land), which is what closes that hole; the trunk-only authority contract sheds to SR-170. The two fail independently: a perfectly serialized landing that lets a lane commit a regenerated artifact satisfies this row and not that one. Terminal close is SR-144's and is not restated here — one SR per (need, property), and both rows sit under SN-027 — so what this row carries of it is only that a terminal branch still lands through the one serial seam.

SR-156 itself — no cell of this row changed

TitleThe verdict record: what carries a governing verdict, and what binds it to the tree it judged
RationaleThe merge gate asks whether a lane may merge while the loop asks which review phase the lane still owes. A shared identity, evidence join, phase span and count keep both answers on the same tree and policy. Logged round evidence is authoritative because its coordinator session establishes reviewer provenance; the self-verifying trailer is only a cross-check and cannot create an approval. Identity binding answers staleness directly and prevents ordering from promoting a verdict for different work. Separate read-only and reset peels protect both contracts: the reader may traverse identity-preserving commits, while the destructive reset path may remove only the verified refresh at its tip.

LLR-IDLLR-262
SR-RefsSR-154;SR-156
TitleThe record a merge judges stays as its author committed it
RationaleEvery merge is judged from records - the verdict files, the Done-when checklist - and each of them could be changed by the lane being judged: a reviewer committing work beside its verdict, a later session rewriting an earlier round, a builder rewording its own Done-when. Nothing on a commit reliably names its role, but the coordinator's session log does, so the checks key on it and are re-derived at merge from what was committed rather than trusted from the loop. Reading a round as its session committed it removes the rewrite without adding a refusal. The Done-when claim rule warns rather than refuses because the intake mint still files its derived rows without a Done-when, and a refusal would park every one of them.

SR-RefsSR-156
TitleA Done-when blessing binds the exact changed checklist
RationaleThe same changed checklist is consulted at dispatch, hand close, merge and adjudication. One digest and covering predicate keep readers from accepting different texts or treating a verdict about an earlier text as a release.
TestRefsTC-325, TC-326
StatusAccepted
Phase6
LLR LLR-339changed
accepted — re-acceptance owed
Detail 13% of the words changed
The integration branch is named by one declaration in the policy home, read by every reader that needs the trunk. A checkout on that branch is the trunk whether it is the primary checkout or a linked worktree. The trunk is never inferred from the branch a checkout is on; an absent, non-text or empty declaration, or one that names no local branch, is refused naming the declaration by every reader that judges or writes against the trunk. With an undeclared trunk, path-triggered step selection reads the change as unknown and runs every opted-in step, the readability report skips each declared measure naming the declaration, and the worker preflight's terminal-row check keeps its guard. A scaffold writes the branch it is on into the declaration only in a policy configuration created in the same scaffold run, never an adopter's existing file; in an unborn repository it writes the branch git will create, and when the destination has no repository or is detached, the template's value stays and the scaffold names the line to set. A lane's integration base is its merge base with the declared trunk, in a single checkout as in a linked worktree.worktree; with no merge base, as on a history sharing no commit with the trunk, it is the checkout's own tip, and a checkout with no commit has none.

Phase6

SR-157Spine and work-registry rules fail the harness verdictacceptance owed

Requirement

The delivered harness shall report every declared spine and work-registry rule violation — broken joins, integrity and schema faults, leftover placeholders, requirement-form findings and work-item coherence contradictions — naming the at-fault row and cell, gating the declared failure set at the declared gate while advisory classes never change the exit code.

Rationale

Realizes SN-002 (the chain is mechanically verified, not asserted — this row is the boundary-level home of the verdict SN-002's acceptance asks for), SN-012 (optional registries and opt-outs cost a non-user nothing) and SN-025 (the tracked work-item graph the loop derives its next work from is kept coherent). One row because the adopter-observable contract is one: registry rules produce named findings at a declared severity; which rules, at which severities, is the component detail the rows below it decompose. Fan-out re-stamp: the delivered contract is one — a named, row-and-cell-attributed finding at a declared severity, gating or advisory — while the rule inventory it ranges over is open by this row's own acceptance ("a rule added at one of those sites is in this row's scope by default"), so the child count tracks the number of declared rules rather than the number of decisions this row makes, and would climb back through any bound a split bought.

SR-157 itself — no cell of this row changed

The declaration this is read against is the rule inventory the delivered checkers themselves publish — the id shapes, the required cells and the closed vocabularies (with the interface and component entries advisory by their own recorded ruling), the strict flag families and their stated exit contracts, the gating form and provenance findings beside the never-gating paraphrase and acceptance advisories, and the work-item coherence rules with their declared opt-out — each recorded at its declaration site, not restated here. A rule added at one of those sites is in this row's scope by default: an unreported one is a finding against this row, not an amendment to it.

LLR-ID
LLR-421
SR-Refs
SR-157
Title
An interface whose reach through the rows constraining it and the cases naming it holds no executable test case is reported
Detail
An interface's reach is the design rows and system requirements whose constrains cell names it, the design rows and requirements a test case naming it verifies, and every test case naming it or verifying one of those rows, a constraining design row's cases including those of the design rows below it in the requirement tree, as a constraining requirement's include those of the design rows decomposing it. Each live interface whose reach holds no test case the harness runs, an observation case being one it does not run, is named on one warning line, and those whose reach holds observation cases alone are named on a line of their own. A count line gives the interfaces reached through a constraining design row, through a constraining requirement, through a naming test case alone, by observation alone, and not at all. The process settings' interface-check opt-out silences the report, and with no live interface row it is empty. The report does not change the strict check's exit code.
Rationale
Every interface is pointed at by a requirement stating its behaviour or by a test case observing it, and reaches a test the harness runs that way; reporting the reach before it fails lets the population land first.

LLR LLR-422Drafted — never accepted

No cell differs from the accepted snapshot. This row is here because its own Status asks for an operator, not because its text moved.

LLR-ID
LLR-422
SR-Refs
SR-157
Title
A test case naming an interface every row it verifies already constrains is reported
Detail
For each interface a test case's verifies cell names, the name repeats each verified design row or system requirement whose constrains cell includes that interface. The name is reported, naming the case, the interface and the rows, only when it repeats every design row and system requirement the case verifies: a case verifying neither repeats nothing, and a case verifying one row that constrains the interface beside a row or requirement that does not repeats nothing. The report changes the exit code of neither the strict check nor the integrity check.
Rationale
A test case names an interface only where it observes the behaviour at a different interface from the one its rows constrain; per pair, a row that constrains nothing still needs the name for its placement.

SR-168The state view shows current progress and next workre-accept owed

Requirement

The delivered generator set shall produce one state view from which a reviewer reads the adopted repository's completeness, its requirement decomposition, its planned and in-flight work, and any declared hold on that work, without consulting a second surface.

Rationale

Realizes SN-023's FIRST conjunct — a reviewer sees the project's progress from one dashboard-like file — with SN-010 (what the reviewer reads there is the generated truth, never a hand-kept summary of it). ONE DECISION PER ROW: the integrity of a generated artifact (SR-070) and what that artifact must SHOW are two obligations that fail independently. This row is the progress half; SR-169 is the connection half, along the conjunction SN-023 itself draws. Deliberately NOT folded back into SR-070: a state view that regenerates deterministically, offline and drift-checkably while omitting the work in flight satisfies that row completely and this one not at all. Deliberately NOT folded into SR-169 either: a complete progress view can carry no seam graph whatsoever — the state this repository was in before its interface registry existed — so the two are separately observable and separately buildable. `phase` inherits the parent's 1 rather than tracking its children (which span 1 through 4), because a phase records WHEN a piece shipped, and child-later-than-parent is the ordinary case.

SR-170Shared authority surfaces are the serial actor's alonere-accept owed

Requirement

The delivered loop content shall write the shared records it derives - the compiled activity log and the generated project-state artifacts - only from the serial merge step and only against the merged tree, never from a parallel work branch.

Rationale

ONE DECISION PER ROW: a shared serial actor is a shared MECHANISM, not one decision. This row holds the EXCLUSIVE-WRITER contract alone; ordering-and-all-or-nothing is SR-173's and identity allocation SR-174's, each with its own children. Realizes SN-027's serialization guarantee at the surfaces where a parallel writer would actually corrupt something, with SN-010 (the status surface a human reads regenerates at the seam, never on a lane), SN-008 (a first failure stops loudly rather than half-regenerating) and SN-025 (the surface a session reads is generated, never hand-copied). The serial LANDING (SR-156) and the rule that shared authority surfaces regenerate only there are two contracts that fail independently, since a perfectly serialized landing that still lets a lane commit a regenerated artifact satisfies the landing row and not this one. The three surfaces sit in ONE row rather than three because they share one decision, not three: the serial trunk actor is their sole writer. That decision is already ruled for the identity mint — a work branch never mints a work-item id — and this row is its requirement-tier home, which it did not have. `phase` inherits the parent's 4, matching every child.

SR-173Shared regeneration is ordered and commits no partial resultre-accept owed

Requirement

The delivered loop content shall regenerate the shared derived artifacts in declared dependency order, stopping at the first failure and committing no partially regenerated set.

Rationale

THE TRANSACTIONALITY HALF of three obligations that fail independently: ordering and all-or-nothing are one decision about a regeneration run, and they are not the same decision as who is allowed to write (SR-170) or how identity is allocated (SR-174). A regeneration that runs in the right order and still commits half a set satisfies neither, which is what makes them separable. Realizes SN-008 - a first failure that stops loudly beats a half-regenerated set that reads as complete - and SN-010. THE GUARANTEE IS "COMMITS NO PARTIAL SET" RATHER THAN "LEAVES NONE BEHIND", and the narrower wording is the one the delivered behaviour supports: the regeneration step touches git NOT AT ALL, so on a first failure it exits nonzero having run no later step, with HEAD unmoved and nothing committed - and it DELIBERATELY LEAVES the already-passing steps' output dirty in the working tree, because the design assigns the commit to the caller (LLR-142's "never commits; the caller owns the commit"). The residue is not tolerated, it is REQUIRED: TC-170's own evidence test asserts `git status --porcelain` is NON-EMPTY after the failure, so an implementation that cleaned up after itself would fail the test that holds this row. The transactional rollback a reader might otherwise expect exists, but it belongs to the WRAPPING callers (the integrator and intake each reset the branch to its last work commit on failure) and is their obligation, not this row's - which is why stating it here would credit t… [60 more chars — read the registry row]

LLR LLR-137ADDED since the snapshot
LLR-ID
LLR-137
SR-Refs
SR-170;SR-173
Title
Serial trunk step (log compile + regen)
Detail
Compiles the session log fragments into the log, appending each through the log's shared appender, in the order of the commits that added them, oldest first, with the file name breaking a tie. Every fragment is validated first: its name shape, an opening second-level heading, a reserved log section heading refused, and an adding commit required, on the checked-out history or, while the integrator composes a merge, on the branch being merged. Relative links are rebased from the fragment directory to the log's, fragments are deleted after the append, and the compile is all-or-nothing on any validation error, a refused compile leaving the log unchanged and every fragment in place. Dotfiles and the directory's own readme are not fragments and are never removed. Never commits; the caller owns the commit. The artifact-regen half is LLR-142. A fragment-free run is a clean idempotent no-op. Asked for the compile, the regeneration, or neither, which runs both with the compile first since it can move the log the generators read, the step runs exactly those; a dry run prints what each selected operation would do and writes nothing. The step exits 0 when every selected operation finished or found nothing to do, and 1 on any failure, naming the offending fragment or generator on its error stream; a failed compile stops the step before the regeneration.
Rationale
One serial actor per §5.5.

SR-178Text that has moved away from its acceptance record is reportedre-accept owed

Requirement

The kit shall report any recorded artifact whose text has moved away from the copy recording its acceptance - stakeholder needs included - regardless of any Status movement.

Rationale

THE DRIFT half, separable from SR-140 in the direction that matters: a record that exists and rides its acceptance commit, with nothing ever comparing live text against it, satisfies SR-140 completely and leaves every later amendment invisible. That is not a hypothetical - it is precisely the failure the git-history derivation had, and the reason a baseline on disk was owed at all. REGARDLESS OF STATUS MOVEMENT is the load-bearing clause, not a qualifier. The amendment machinery watches the Status flip, and the sanctioned staged-amendment path deliberately does not flip - so a rule keyed off the flip returns nothing on exactly the rows a sitting exists to judge. Reporting against the recorded copy is the only reading that survives that, because the copy is a baseline OUTSIDE the live file and a walk over the live file can never be one. THE STAKEHOLDER-NEED RIDER is in the normative text rather than left to the acceptance because the need tier is the one a sitting most wants to hold and the one the amendment-watching rules were last to reach: each rule walks the tiers it is given, so without the clause the need tier is left off the walk and no rule reaches it. Needs are compared row by row under the same accepted and traced split as every other tier. WHAT COUNTS AS MOVEMENT is one definition with one home, shared with the amend-without-flip guard: the id column and the Status cell are excluded STRUCTURALLY (the id is the join key rather than content, and folding Status in woul… [817 more chars — read the registry row]

SR-178 itself — no cell of this row changed
SR-IDSR-178
TitleText that has moved away from its acceptance record is reported
SN-RefsSN-029
Boundary-RefsB-02
Hat-RefsTEST-ENGINEER
AcceptanceCriteriaAn artifact whose normative text has moved since the copy that recorded its acceptance is reported whether or not its Status moved; an accepted cell or an adjudicated link moving under a row still claiming acceptance is reported, while a trace cell no adjudicator judges moving is not, and a row below acceptance has no claim to fall from and is not reported; the status marker itself is not read as the movement, so a flip is neither an amendment nor a mask for one; and stakeholder needs, assumptions and surrogates are held to the same rule.

LLR LLR-359changed
Detail 52% of the words changed
A row's accepted-text digest is the identity of the text a sitting judges on it, formed from its nonempty accepted cells and its nonempty adjudicated links (LLR-484), excluding its id and Status: carrier representation and an absent versus empty cell give the same identity, a moved accepted cell or adjudicated link changes it, and a moved trace cell no adjudicator judges does not. An amendment brief renders one judged-text identity line for each row it shows for judgement.judgement, together as one block. A composed brief of any kind whose text reads as identity lines other than exactly the ones its composer wrote, as one block naming each row once, is refused at composition, naming the row each differing line names and where it sits: a line of that shape the composer did not write, whether in a shown row's cell, in a later section of a combined sitting or anywhere else in its text; a line the composer wrote that no longer reads as one, its template having moved the identity slot off the start of a line; or lines that are not one block naming each row once. A brief whose identity block ends its text is refused too, so a binding records exactly the identity lines the composer wrote. Reservation writes thosethe identities of that one block in the binding, and rewriting the binding as recorded or failed preserves them.them; it refuses, naming each line and writing no binding, a brief carrying a line of that shape outside one block of distinct rows, or any such line when it requests no amendment, so text added to a composed brief after its composition can neither add an identity nor replace one. A held CLARITY re-acceptance is actable only when the accepted binding's identity equals the committed row's digest, and an absent, malformed or unequal identity yields a refusal before launch naming the row, an unequal one naming both digests; a held acceptance claim is excepted only under a CLARITY verdict whose binding records the row's equal digest. The contract covers registries, recorded copies and bindings the kit's own routes write; a hand-edited recorded copy, act ledger or binding is outside it.
Rationale 65% of the words changed
A verdict about one text cannot authorize a later text unless the text the verdict saw has a stable identity carried from brief reservation through the later act. The adjudicated links are in the identity because an act naming the row records them with its accepted text (LLR-245), so a link moved after the verdict would otherwise be recorded under a verdict that never judged it. Comparing the committed identity with the accepted binding keeps a changed, absent or malformed identity from being read as the judged row. Only the composer's own identity lines may reach the binding because a judged cell is shown verbatim and may span lines, and a brief carries other text that is not the composer's (a later section, a spec's context, a template, and any section appended to it before reservation): a line of the identity's shape there would add an identity no shown text has or, after the genuine line, replace one, and a held act could then take a row whose text the verdict never judged. Composition refuses such a brief, keeping judged text verbatim and closing every source of its text at one point; reservation, which reads the brief as a route hands it, binds only one block of distinct rows, so a line added after composition is refused too, a block ending a brief is refused so an appended line cannot join it, and a composer's line its template hides from the reader is refused so an appended line cannot stand in its place.

Method 51% of the words changed
Digest a requirement row, then the same row with its id, its Status and a trace cell no adjudicator judges changed, with an empty cell against an absent one, and on its other carrier; then with an accepted cell moved, and with an adjudicated link moved. Compose an amendment brief over committed rows whose accepted cells moved, and one over a row whose adjudicated link alone moved, and read their judged-text lines. Reserve a sitting from a composed prompt, round-trip and read its binding, and rewrite it as recorded and as failed. Derive the held act of a CLARITY verdict whose binding equals the committed row, one whose row's accepted cell moved after it, one whose row's adjudicated link moved after it, one whose binding was written over the row's accepted cells alone, one with no identity and one with a malformed identity cell, reading each refusal for the row and, where the identities are unequal, for the digest the binding records and the committed row's digest; and launch the act turn over one whose text moved. Judge a held acceptance claim under a CLARITY verdict binding its equal digest, and one whose adjudicated link moved after the verdict. Compose an amendment brief whose shown row's moved cell, a first-acceptance brief whose awaiting row's cell, and a combined sitting whose later first-acceptance section's row cell holds a line of the judged-text identity's shape, each in turn naming that row, another row the brief shows and a row it does not show; a combined sitting whose Done-when section's context holds one naming the amendment section's row; a combined sitting whose own template holds one; every other routed brief kind with one of its slots holding one, and each again without it; an amendment brief whose template ends with its identity block; one whose template moves its identity slot off the start of a line, and the same brief with an identity line appended before a sitting is reserved from it; and one whose composer's identity lines are split apart or name a row twice. Compose the amendment, first-acceptance and combined briefs again without the line, read their judged-text lines and reserve a sitting from each. Reserve a sitting from a composed combined brief with a section appended holding such a line, from a brief requesting no amendment with an appended section holding one, and from a brief whose one block names a row twice.

Phase6

SR-208Held-rung changes are refused before they landre-accept owed

Requirement

Where the declared acceptance level holds a snapshotted registry's rung for an operator, the delivered harness shall refuse, before it lands on trunk, both a lane's acceptance claim that is not a CLARITY re-acceptance bound to the row's judged text and a marked autonomous commit outside a lane that moves a held status or makes a held acceptance claim.

Rationale

The acceptance level names the rungs an operator still accepts, and an automated acceptance of the spine's own rows is already refused. The frame, assumption, surrogate and component registries carry status cells of their own that no automated path was stopped from changing, because the one setting that would stop it defaults to allowing. A policy that declares a rung operator-held without refusing the automated path is a claim, not a control. A lane can carry an acceptance into trunk even when no automated writer made its commit, and an autonomous commit outside a lane can make the same change; protecting only selected writers leaves the held state dependent on who delivered it. Binding the permitted re-acceptance to its judged text prevents a verdict about one text authorizing another, while leaving an unmarked reviewed trunk acceptance to its operator authority.

SR-208 itself — no cell of this row changed
SR-IDSR-208
TitleHeld-rung changes are refused before they land
SN-RefsSN-029
Boundary-RefsB-02
Hat-RefsSECURITY
AcceptanceCriteriaWith a snapshotted registry's rung held, a lane acceptance claim is refused naming the row, its rung and the dial unless the claim is a re-acceptance whose live status is unchanged and accepting on both sides, whose committed live row equals its copy, and whose accepted amendment verdict rules that row CLARITY and binds its equal accepted-text digest; a live status flip, born acceptance, copied acceptance-cell or status movement, or copied-row retirement is a claim, while a Drafted sibling, a demotion, a below-acceptance move, a traced-only copy move, a released rung and an example row are not judged; every lane commit is judged against each parent at the merge slot and a squash landing is judged by its lane commits; an unmarked plain trunk commit is not judged; a marked autonomous commit outside a lane that moves a held status or makes a held acceptance claim is refused naming the row, rung and dial; an unreadable or unparseable live registry or copy is refused by name; with the rung released the change proceeds; a registry whose rung cannot be read is treated as held, so a refused change never lands on trunk.

Phase6

SR-209Every commit the loop makes carries a checked provenance trailerre-accept owed

Requirement

When a commit is made from a process the unattended loop started, or is merged in a lane the loop owns from the point the loop took that lane over, the delivered commit floor shall refuse it unless it carries a valid provenance trailer naming the loop and its session.

Rationale

A status change on an operator-held rung can be told apart from an operator's only if every loop-made commit says it is one. Without a floor that holds every loop commit to it, a trailer could appear on only some of the loop's commits and a missing one would prove nothing. Where a commit is made, the condition is who made it, not when: a person committing in another checkout while the loop runs is not the loop. Where a lane merges, the floor sees commits, not processes, so it reads whose lane it is from the commits and judges every commit of a loop lane, a person's included: there an unmarked commit the loop made cannot be told from a person's, and a person's commit belongs in the person's own lane. The commits made before the loop took a lane over are exempt, since nothing then could have marked them. Refusing the unmarked commit at the floor makes the mark total, which is what lets a later check treat its absence as meaningful. The floor where a commit is made is local and a determined bypass defeats it, which is why the merge re-checks a loop lane and the history check stands behind both.

SR-209 itself — no cell of this row changed
SR-IDSR-209
TitleEvery commit the loop makes carries a checked provenance trailer
SN-RefsSN-029;SN-005
Boundary-RefsB-01;B-04
Hat-RefsSECURITY;UNATTENDED-OPS
AcceptanceCriteriaEvery process the loop starts carries a marker the commit floor reads, so a commit made from a loop-started process is known as the loop's; such a commit with no trailer, or with a malformed one, is refused naming the commit's subject; a commit made by a process the loop did not start needs no trailer and passes where it is made, even while the loop is running; the same validity rule, a trailer that is present and well-formed, applies wherever the commit floor runs. Where the floor judges a lane's commits at its merge, it reads whose lane it is from the commits: a lane is the loop's when its claim, or any commit one of the loop's own writers made in its range, carries the trailer. Every commit of a loop lane is then judged, whoever runs the merge, so a person's commit there must carry the trailer or move to the person's own lane; the commits before the lane's first trailer-carrying loop-writer commit are exempt from this check; a lane that is not the loop's is not judged. An attended re-claim recorded in the lane, a commit that changes nothing against its one parent, names that lane in the re-claim form, and carries no provenance trailer at all, ends the loop's window for the commits after it; a commit that fails any of those conditions is judged like any other; a later loop-writer commit re-opens the window.

TitleThe loop provenance trailer and the loop marker
DetailThe loop's provenance rule lives in the kit's shared package, which imports no sibling script. It declares the commit-message trailer Loop-Session and the loop marker, one environment variable naming the session. A session's trailer is rendered as one trailer line, and parsing a message returns its last trailer's session or none. The refusal names the commit's subject when the trailer is missing, malformed, or names another session. Given a commit message file, the harness's loop-trailer check exits 0 when the marker is not set or the refusal finds nothing, and exits 1, printing the refusal, when the marker is set and it does. The dispatcher's run and the loop's main entry, outside interactive mode, set the marker in their own process environment once per run, never in a launcher script, so child sessions inherit it and a person's shell does not.

Phase6

SR-215At a checkpoint, an unjudged, expired or triggered observation test is queued once for re-judgingre-accept owed

Requirement

When a work item merges, a release is prepared or a stage gate is checked, the delivered harness shall file one re-judge work item per due observation case: one with no result, one whose result expired, or one whose declared trigger fires after its work-item floor; with no declared trigger, a change to its declared inputs makes it due, subject to that floor. It keeps at most one open item per case, and reports each observation case that cites no written pass criteria as a warning.

Rationale

Judgements cost time and model calls and vary across sessions. Written pass criteria fixed before the first judgement make the pass reviewable. The closed-work floor and the declared trigger are cost limits the operator directed (the PERFORMANCE lens): no change or checkpoint makes an accepted judgement due again within the configured number of closed work items of its latest record, and a declared trigger replaces input changes with the change or checkpoint it names, so a judgement can stand on changes it never judged until a qualifying change or checkpoint meets the floor or its result expires. First judgement and expiry keep missing or old evidence from standing indefinitely.

SR-215 itself — no cell of this row changed
SR-IDSR-215
TitleAt a checkpoint, an unjudged, expired or triggered observation test is queued once for re-judging
SN-RefsSN-024;SN-008;SN-043
Boundary-RefsB-01
Hat-RefsTEST-ENGINEER;PERFORMANCE
AcceptanceCriteriaA case with no result is due at once, and a case whose result expired is due at once; neither waits for the floor. Otherwise a declared trigger (matching changed files, a change to a module tagged with the named component, release preparation, or a stage-gate check) makes a case due only once the configured number of closed work items has passed since its latest record was committed; a case may raise that number but not lower it. With no trigger, a change to its declared inputs makes it due, subject to the same floor; with neither a trigger nor inputs, only absence and expiry make it due. Inputs and policy are read at the checkpoint revision. An open re-judge item suppresses a second, and the decision runs no model. An observation case that cites no written pass criteria is reported as a warning, never a failure, including under strict checking.

CoincidentThe needs ask that an accepted observation not stand on a state it never judged, and that it be judged against written criteria. Filing one re-judge item for each case that is unjudged, expired or triggered past its floor, and warning on a case that cites no criteria, is that outcome within the cost bound the floor sets. The judging itself is the re-judge item's own.

TitleThe merge-slot intake re-run for a merge made outside the slot
RationaleA merge made outside the slot - a person squash-merging a lane and closing its spec on trunk - owes the same rows the slot mints, and a skipped mint is silent: the re-judge rows, the dispositions and the follow-ups a verdict drafted never exist. SR-220Overlapping queued work is consolidated through one judgement per queue statere-accept owed

Requirement

While the queued work items include a set that overlaps, the delivered loop content shall hand that set to a single judgement, at most once for any one state of the queued work items and never while another judgement is in progress, queued over an item of the set, or queued to run before it, whose outcome takes effect only as a recorded restructuring in which each absorbed item is closed in a terminal state naming its one successor, its scope text unchanged.

Rationale

A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-025 asks that no human curate what comes next; its acceptance states only that the next work derives from the tracked work-item graph, which a stranger reading it would derive as work selection alone. This row states what that promise needs once the loop also mints work: rows minted one finding at a time overlap, and without this row either a human merges them, which is the curation the need rules out, or the loop builds the same job more than once. Two lenses shape it. UNATTENDED-OPS: an overlap is a standing state, so a judgement fired on it re-asks the same question on every idle tick unless the judged state is remembered past the judging row's close, an unbounded retry that pages nobody. PERFORMANCE: each judgement is a strong-tier session, so asking once per queue state is the operating-cost bound rather than an optimisation. The restructuring is recorded rather than done by deletion so that an absorbed item's scope and its successor stay readable after the act. Fed back to the need: SN-025's acceptance could name the loop keeping its own queue free of duplicated work; until it does, this row is derived and says so. Choosing the next work stays SR-148's, and reporting an overlap as a warning stays SR-157's.

SR-220 itself — no cell of this row changed

TitleOverlapping queued work is consolidated through one judgement per queue state

Hat-RefsUNATTENDED-OPS;PERFORMANCE
AcceptanceCriteriaA queue holding an overlapping set yields exactly one judgement row, and the same state of the queued work items yields none again, including after that judgement has closed; no judgement is started while another is in progress, while another consolidation is pending, while a queued judgement names an item of the set, or while a queued judgement would run before it, each refusal naming that judgement, and a queued judgement that does neither holds nothing back; a judgement is never itself a member of a set; a set is never seeded by, and never re-absorbs, an item created by an earlier judgement that enacted a consolidation, while an item made by a judgement that consolidated nothing, or by a consolidation without a recorded judgement, is not treated as judged; an outcome that moves an item no longer queued is refused, naming the item; an absorbing outcome leaves each absorbed item in a terminal state naming its one successor with its scope text unchanged, and the successor records every item it supersedes.

SR-222Every model session is recorded in one usage record, whichever provider CLI serves itre-accept owed

Requirement

The delivered loop content shall record every model session it launches, whichever provider command-line runner serves it, in one usage record: token usage under a published usage vocabulary pinned to a named revision, input counted inclusive of cached input, fresh input derived by one formula, the runner's raw usage kept verbatim, the route's provider filled from its roster row for every routed call, the runner named, the vocabulary's gen_ai.provider.name holding the runner's default provider for claude and codex and empty for any other runner, with runner reconfiguration to another provider not detected, and the context occupancy of the session's latest request kept apart from its billed tokens; for a runner other than claude, codex and opencode, the runner name is left empty, and so is every count or raw usage the loop cannot read from the runner's output.

Rationale

A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-026 asks that the operator can configure several model families and route work among them per job and capability level; it does not name a usage record, so this obligation arrives through two lenses rather than through the need's text. The performance lens listens for measurements that cannot be compared, or are unavailable, being read as a reliable total: routing among families is a cost and quality choice, and the choice is blind when one family's calls record their usage and another's do not, or when the runners disagree on what "input tokens" means (two count cached input apart from input, one counts it inside), so one column would hold two incompatible numbers. The consistency lens listens for readers disagreeing after a one-sided change: when each launching role keeps its own launch and logging path, a usage fix lands in one path and not the others. A published vocabulary avoids inventing a log format for a problem that is not novel; because it has no tagged release, the record pins the exact revision it follows. The raw usage is kept verbatim so a mapping later found wrong is re-derived rather than lost. Occupancy is kept apart from billed tokens because they answer different questions: billed tokens are summed over every request the session made, while how full a context is depends on the latest request alone, and a session reset that read the first as the second would fire on sessions that are nowhere near full. A runner who… [376 more chars — read the registry row]

SR-223A guarded session receives the guardrails payload vendored for its modelre-accept owed

Requirement

Where a repository vendors a guardrails payload for a model-name substring, the delivered loop content shall inject that payload, in place of the default guardrails core, into a guarded session whose model name contains that substring.

Rationale

A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-026 asks that models be selected per job and capability level; it does not name what a session on a given model is told, so this obligation arrives through the unattended-operations lens rather than through the need's text. A guardrails posture written for one model is ritual noise to a stronger one and too little for a weaker one, and in an unattended run nobody watches a session drift: one core for every guarded model is the silent degrade that lens listens for. Selecting by a substring of the model name reuses the matcher the guardrails policy already applies, so one grammar decides both whether a session is guarded and what it is given, and the repository's own file names carry the mapping. A per-model key in the policy dial was the alternative, and it lost because a model name in shared configuration is the name that rots when models turn over. Fed back to the need: SN-026's acceptance could name a per-model posture; until it does, this row is derived and says so.

SR-223 itself — no cell of this row changed

Hat-RefsUNATTENDED-OPS
AcceptanceCriteriaA guarded session on a model whose name contains a vendored payload's substring receives that payload and not the default core; when several vendored substrings match, the longest wins; a session on a model matching none receives the default core; a session the declared guardrails policy leaves unguarded receives nothing, whatever payloads are vendored; the kit ships no payload of its own, and so names no model to the selection: with only the default core present, every model receives the default core.

SR-225A delegated run closes with a record of the calls it made, where the declared dial asks for onere-accept owed

Requirement

The delivered loop content shall keep the operator's verdict on each delegated decision coupled to the work that acts on it and each delegated run to its record: whatever the declared decision-recording dial, reporting without refusing a record entry that omits a required disclosure field or leaves one blank, treating each run's record as the listing the operator reads, in which an entry with no verdict is not yet seen, and refusing an overrule unless its transition change files or amends open work that cites the entry; and, where that dial asks for a record, refusing to integrate a closing lane whose record is absent, naming where it belongs.

Rationale

A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-029 asks that a run released to automation get as far as it honestly can, and that an acceptance it makes on a released tier leave a record naming who made it; it does not name the other calls a delegated run makes on the operator's behalf, the ones too settled to hold the run for and not settled enough to be history, so this obligation arrives through the unattended-operations lens rather than through the need's text. A call nobody is told about is the failure that lens listens for: it pages nobody, and a run that seems to pass does so partly because nothing looked at what it chose. Required fields and a close refusal make that record durable rather than an instruction that can be omitted. The obligation is keyed to a dial because how much of the operator's reading a run may claim is the operator's to set; it ships off, so a repository owes nothing until its owner asks. A malformed entry is reported rather than refused, because the record is there and readable and a refusal would hold finished work for a reporting defect. SR-227Where the retention dial is on, adjudication routes retain sessions and reset them only when safere-accept owed

Requirement

Where the declared adjudicator retention dial is above zero, the delivered adjudication content shall manage each retained-class adjudication consistently whether the coordinator or loop starts it: resume the route's active, unheld session in its model runner's resume form; mint and record a session id where the route has no active session; wait a bounded time where its active session is held, then run unretained and state why; record each call like any other; drain the session when its latest request's occupancy reaches the dial or the inputs it judges under change; retire it only when no work it has a stake in is pending, except that at the first keep after a blackout window it retires a session without a last use or last used before that window ended with reason blackout even when its chain is pending; retire it at once when a call fails except that an authentication failure records the failed call and leaves its session record unchanged; let no two calls use one retained session at once; write the retention state only whole, by one writer at a time; make any keep-warm call one bounded turn that never blocks the scheduler; compose both routes' retention request once; and, before retaining a family that uses a dedicated CLI home, read any long-lived credential at each launch from the location a declared environment setting names, keep every verbatim occurrence out of logs, records and commits, and refuse an absent or unreadable credential before launch, naming setup without… [336 more chars — read the registry row]

Rationale

A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-025 asks that a configured agent implement toward the vision with no human curating what comes next; it does not name retaining a session, so this obligation arrives through three lenses rather than through the need's text. The performance lens listens for an operating-cost risk left unassessed: a run that spins a fresh adjudicator for every small work item reloads the spine on each call, and the usage record makes that cost visible, while resuming a session by id uses the provider's prompt cache. The unattended-operations lens listens for a silent degrade: a retained session that has filled, judges under changed rules, or is started in a home whose sign-in cannot be read degrades every later judgement with nothing paging anyone; a coordinator route that bypasses the retained operation repeats that cost and loses the state the loop preserves. One composition keeps both routes under the same drain and lease rules. The integrity lens listens for a durable artifact updated in place without an all-or-nothing guarantee and for a claim nothing can reclaim: two calls resuming one transcript at once corrupt it, so one call at a time holds a session, under a hold that expires when its holder is gone. Refusing an unreadable or absent sign-in before that hold prevents a failed retained launch from changing state or escaping into a person's default home. Retiring only when no work the session has a stake in is pending keeps a review, rework and … [503 more chars — read the registry row]

TitleCoordinator adjudication shares the keep request and refuses an unsigned account

Phase6

SR-229Coordinator claim admission respects a blackout pausere-accept owed

Requirement

Where a blackout window is armed, the delivered work-item admission shall refuse a new claim inside that window on every route at any context-guard dial; outside a blackout window, where the declared coordinator context guard is enabled, it admits a coordinator claim only from the current lease holder while that holder's context drain is not latched, the drain latching once the holder's context occupancy reaches the declared threshold and holding across compaction and resume until the operator records a clear or a release, the holder records a hand-back naming its handoff, or a relaunched successor takes the lease.

Rationale

A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-027 asks that a declared pause stop claiming and drain what is already in flight, and that recovery never double-assign work; it does not name the coordinating session's own context, so this obligation arrives through two lenses rather than through the need's text. The unattended-operations lens listens for a silent degrade: a coordinator whose context has crossed its declared limit keeps claiming after compaction has degraded the context it needs to close the work it already holds, and nothing pages anyone. The integrity lens listens for a recovery path written only for the unattended run when the same corruption is reachable attended, and for a claim nothing can reclaim once its holder is gone: a retained adjudicator session already drains on occupancy while the attended coordinator does not, and a second or missing holder leaves claim authority ambiguous, so one lease names the coordinator and only the operator's recorded release frees a lease whose holder has gone. A latched single-holder admission boundary was chosen over a one-time event observation because every claim must remain protected when an event is missed or the transcript later reports a lower reading. Fed back to the need: SN-027's acceptance could name the coordinating session's own capacity as a declared reason to stop claiming and drain; until it does, this row is derived and says so.

SR-229 itself — no cell of this row changed
SR-IDSR-229
TitleCoordinator claim admission respects a blackout pause
SN-RefsSN-027
Boundary-RefsB-09;B-10
Hat-RefsINTEGRITY-RECOVERABILITY;UNATTENDED-OPS
AcceptanceCriteriaInside an armed blackout window, every claim route, including the unattended dispatcher, refuses a new claim naming the window end at any dial; outside it, with the guard off, admission proceeds without reading or writing guard state; with it on, the holder below the threshold may claim, while a claim with no lease held, a claim from another session or from a caller naming no session, and a claim from a holder at or above the threshold each refuse, naming the recovery action. The unattended dispatcher's own admission remains available without consulting this guard. Occupancy counts the newest valid post-compaction usage on the live branch, including cached input, and unknown usage is not zero. A latched drain survives a lower reading, compaction and resume, and ends only when the operator records a clear or a release, the holder records a hand-back naming its handoff, or a relaunched successor takes the lease. With the guard off, a hand-back reads and writes no guard state. Only the holder may record a hand-back, which names a handoff carrying a session prompt; another or unnamed caller is refused, naming the operator's release, whatever handoff it names; the holder's promptless handoff or own pending relaunch request is refused; a relaunch request left by a previous holder does not block the hand-back; and a subsequent session may take the freed lease.

SR-Refs
SR-230;SR-229
Title
Coordinator relaunch request and exit acquisition
Detail
The session-prompt reading extracts the one fenced session prompt from a handoff, preserving headings inside that fence as prompt text. The relaunch request refuses inside a blackout window and otherwise accepts only the lease holder and an existing handoff, then writes a same-session request atomically. Inside a blackout window the hook's session-end handling cancels a pending request and records blackout; otherwise the session-end handling acts only for a true exit by that holder in its declared repository, rejects a foreign request, and atomically renames one request to acquire it and holds the store lock from that acquisition until the launch is confirmed or the request restored, so restoration never waits on the lock. It writes a fresh successor token into the lease and the handoff prompt to a file, invokes the platform launcher detached in the declared root with that file and token, and on any failure before launch confirmation restores the request and removes the successor token and the prompt file. The launch selects the Windows or POSIX launcher, waits through its grace period and confirms only a still-running launcher or one that exits zero. Each launcher enters the declared root, resolves one interpreter by running its virtual-environment candidate followed by the candidates from its development setup's ordinary runtime search, and takes the first that reports Python 3.11 or later as its executable path; it exits nonzero with guidance when none resolves, runs the g… [854 more chars — read the registry row]
Rationale
The request must be owned, durable and consumed once, because a close-out that launches twice can create two coordinators and a failed launch must leave the handoff recoverable.

SR-230Coordinator successor at session exit outside a blackout pausere-accept owed

Requirement

Where the declared coordinator context guard is enabled, when the coordinator lease holder that has requested a relaunch from its handoff ends its session outside an armed blackout window, the delivered coordination support shall start exactly one successor session from that handoff; inside the window it cancels the pending request and leave resumption to the operator.

Rationale

A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-027 asks that claiming drain what is already in flight and that a crash at any lifecycle boundary recover without double-assignment; it does not name a coordinating session handing over to its successor, so this obligation arrives through the integrity lens rather than through the need's text. That lens asks what the next reader finds when a holder is gone: a close-out that loses its handoff strands the next coordinator, and one that can launch twice creates competing claim authority. An atomic, owned request consumed once was chosen over treating every session-end event as a relaunch because clear and resume do not end the coordinator and a failed launch must remain recoverable. Fed back to the need: SN-027's acceptance could name an orderly handover of the coordinating role as part of a drain; until it does, this row is derived and says so.

SR-230 itself — no cell of this row changed
SR-IDSR-230
TitleCoordinator successor at session exit outside a blackout pause
SN-RefsSN-027
Boundary-RefsB-09;B-10
Hat-RefsINTEGRITY-RECOVERABILITY
AcceptanceCriteriaWith the guard off, no exit launches anything. Outside an armed blackout window, a request from the holder for an existing handoff launches once at an exit in the declared repository and starts from that handoff. Inside the window, no relaunch is requested or launched, and a pending request at the holder's exit is cancelled and recorded as blackout. Clear, resume, a non-holder, a subagent and a foreign request launch nothing; concurrent exit handling consumes one request once; and a launch that fails for any reason restores the request.
